# Verification log — the raw findings of every deep pass, verbatim

**Status:** PROCESS — append-only audit trail. **Never edited, only appended.**

**Why it exists.** `OPEN_GATES.md` holds *state* — what is still open and what closing it means.
It is deliberately curated: one row per distinct defect, rewritten as understanding improves. That
curation loses things. A pass returns a hundred-plus findings from a dozen independent verifiers,
many overlapping, some superseded within the hour — and the standing rule is that **every finding is
written down when it is found** (`LESSONS.md` L-11). This file is where they are written down, in
the verifier's own words, before any judgement is applied.

Read this file to answer *"what did the pass actually say?"*. Read `OPEN_GATES.md` to answer
*"what is still open?"*. If the two ever disagree, this file is the record and the register is the
claim — and the register is the one to fix.

---

## Pass 9 — Phase 7 reverification, 2026-08-30, commit `dfc27fe`

**The debt `OG-190`, `OG-203` and `OG-209` recorded, come due.** Four independent verifiers, each at
the session tier (`fable`), each handed the CLAIM and never the diff, each pinned READ-ONLY to
`dfc27fe` and working in throwaway `/tmp` labs. One refutation round was declared before dispatch;
the fixes below then go to a final round (Phase 7's own fixes) rather than back to these four. The
tree did not move while they ran. Rounds bounded before start per `DEEP_VERIFICATION_PASS.md` §3.3.

**V1 — OG-209 (Phase 6 round-two fixes).** *Claim 1 (L-39, single owner) PARTIALLY REFUTED, MEDIUM:*
the renderer genuinely emits from `STATE_DEFINITIONS` (no `render.py` copy; rebuild byte-identical),
but the module DOCSTRING is a surviving third copy saying `rungs 2-4` while the constant said `rungs
2-3`, and 33 rung-4 nodes are classified `documented` while the definition said `rungs 2-3` — the
L-39 shape one layer up (definition-vs-logic). → `OG-217`, fixed. *Claim 2 (L-40, max-over-history
ratchet) STANDS* on the enforcement path (lower+commit+3 more+rename all caught), with a LOW latent
residual: a shallow clone truncates history with no rewrite and `ratchet_memory` did not report
UNAVAILABLE — off the local-hook path but newly reachable under OG-31's CI (`actions/checkout`
defaults to depth 1). → `OG-218`, fixed.

**V2 — OG-203 (Phase 5 round-two fixes).** *Claim A (surfaces env derivation) REFUTED, HIGH:* three
defects, all the scan-text-not-structure class (`OG-166`/L-24) — a phantom (`ENGINE_VERSION`, a JS
const matched by a shell pattern against `v${ENGINE_VERSION}`), a docstring-satisfied credit
(`ENGINE_CONFIDENTIAL_DIR` → `staged_content_scanner.py`, which reads it only in a docstring), and
invisible constant-name reads (`ENGINE_CONFIG_PATH` via `_CONFIG_PATH_ENV`), plus a CIRCULAR liveness
probe. → `OG-215`, fixed. *Claim C (symbols/gatekeepers resolution) PARTIALLY REFUTED, MEDIUM:*
`gatekeepers._names_module` resolves a module citation by substring, so a COMMENT manufactures a
published `guarded_by` edge (demonstrated). → `OG-216`, fixed (removed 4 real comment-edges on the
live tree). LOW-MEDIUM residual: `symbols._names_or_text`'s text fallback is comment-satisfiable when
the cited module is UNPARSEABLE — a precondition that reddens lint/import/tests, so unreachable on a
green tree. → `OG-219`, NOT_A_DEFECT. *Claims B (moat gatekeepers genuinely enforcing) and D
(determinism, 3 seeds + TZ/locale byte-identical) STAND.*

**V3 — OG-190 (Pass 8 closures), the moat red-team.** The highest-value lane: TWO real breaches at
the new edge of narrowed gates (L-26), neither a rerun of the 21 retained attacks. *BREACH #1,
CRITICAL:* the placement gate `_looks_like_doc` read a header only for nine allowlisted suffixes, so
a `classification: secret` doc as `notes.mkd` (a standard Markdown alias) or with NO extension walked
past both gates to the remote — OG-41's un-swept edge. → `OG-213`, fixed (content decides now).
*BREACH #2, MEDIUM:* pre-commit scans the staged path column (`OG-186`) but pre-push never did, so a
key-shaped FILENAME via a merge (which skips pre-commit) reached the remote at push. → `OG-214`,
fixed via L9 (pre-push now writes `__paths__`). *HELD, strongly:* the secret-CONTENT path — the
additive transcode is robust across wide encodings and object types; the only slips are THREAT_MODEL's
explicitly-disclaimed transforms, which V3 correctly refused to report as false reds.

**V4 — completeness critic.** Attacked the acceptance set itself. Load-bearing findings, all folded
into the closure work: A-6 was trivially green via the HANDOFF amnesty (OG-09 must remove it and
prove the `stamps` guard passes via `body_matches`, not declaration); the OG-31 ruleset creates a
closeout WEDGE (all further pushes must go via a self-merged PR) that must be planned before
activating; `unittest -k` with zero matches exits 0, so A-5's close must capture `Ran N≥1`; the
reverification's own completeness is a process fact (OG-208 shape) that must be recorded as shas +
verdicts, which this entry is; and the honest `verified≥1` path is `moat`/`THREAT_MODEL.md` alone
(the only all-rung-4 container).

**Disposition.** `OG-190`, `OG-203`, `OG-209` are closed by this pass having run — the independent
attack they each asked for. Its residue is `OG-213`…`OG-219`, six fixed and one NOT_A_DEFECT, each
with a retained counter-example. What this pass did NOT do, stated plainly: it did not re-attack each
of Pass 8's ~100 individual closures one by one — it attacked the load-bearing ones (the moat, the
surfaces, the ratchet, the state definitions) and found real defects in every lane, which is the
evidence that the thesis "implementer-verified fixes do not survive an independent attack unchanged"
was correct a fourth time. The final round attacks THESE fixes.

## Pass 8 — Phase 3 deep verification, third run, 2026-08-23, commit `ba0294a`

**14 verifiers dispatched, 13 returned. 129 findings: 4 CRITICAL,
45 HIGH, 53 MEDIUM, 27 LOW.**

Verdicts by lane: PARTLY_REFUTED ×9, REFUTED ×4.

**⚠ The adversarial-refutation round and the completeness critic did NOT run.** 55 of 165 agents
died on a session limit, including every refuter for the CRITICAL findings and the critic itself.
So this pass has **no independent second opinion on its own findings** — each was verified by hand
against the artifact before being acted on, and each fix falsified in a disposable lab, but that is
the implementer checking the verifier, which is exactly the arrangement
`DEEP_VERIFICATION_PASS.md` §1 exists to avoid. **Pass 9 should re-run the refutation round over
this table before treating any unfixed row as true.**

**Aimed, per `NEXT_SESSION.md` §0, at what Passes 6 and 7 both missed** — surfaces nobody has ever
executed, the exemption family as a whole, and the counter-examples themselves. All three lanes
returned defects, and the three worst findings of the pass came from them:

* **`under_dated_heading` was exempting 29.6% of all corpus prose** from five guards and from
  `CONS-06`, which the pre-push moat runs — including three live *policy* documents at 78–88%,
  because each carries a version strapline as a heading. It was my own fix from the previous
  session, and `L-31` — *measure a fix's cost on the real corpus* — is the lesson I wrote and did
  not apply.
* **One leading blank line** hid a `classification: secret` document from both L14 gates.
* **A lightweight tag** pointing at a blob published it unscanned, because the handler fired only
  on the annotated spelling and the retained counter-example covered only that one.

The pass also found, repeatedly, the same *shape* in three unrelated guards: **a ground truth that
ordinary prose can widen** — a fenced table declaring real governance, a fenced heading defining a
register key, a comment satisfying a symbol citation. `test_refs` had that lesson written in its
own docstring and three siblings had it open.

Disposition in `docs/process/OPEN_GATES.md` (`OG-140`…`OG-179`); what changed in `CHANGELOG.md`
`[0.4.3]`. Lessons `L-32`…`L-34`.

---

## Pass 7 — Phase 3 deep verification of the Pass 6 FIXES, 2026-08-22/23, commit `35cd4f8`

**12 agents. 91 findings: 7 CRITICAL, 34 HIGH, 29 MEDIUM, 21 LOW.**

Verdicts by agent: PARTLY_REFUTED ×9, REFUTED ×1.

**This pass attacked the previous pass's fixes, and that is why it found so much.** Every agent was
handed a *claim* — a register row asserting something was closed — and told to break it, with the
live tree read-only and falsification confined to disposable `git archive` labs.

**The dominant shape, in one sentence: five of six Pass-6 narrowings had a hole one step over from
where the fix landed.** `stamps` had been narrowed from a blanket amnesty and re-tested, and still
auto-excused documents through a *second* amnesty two lines below. `CONS-03` had two of its four
exits converted from SKIP to FAIL; a verifier walked through the two that remained and unlocked the
moat's own executable. `OG-56` recorded a UTF-16 hole "closed at the ROOT" and it was closed for one
of two consumers; `OG-84` then fixed both consumers and claimed a counter-example "parametrised over
five encodings — the class, not the instance", and those five codec NAMES covered three wire forms,
omitting the two that a verifier used to commit a `classification: secret` document clean.

That is `LESSONS.md` `L-26` — *a fix that narrows a hole must be attacked at its NEW edge* — and this
pass is the evidence that writing the lesson down is not the same as applying it.

**The single most valuable result** was not any one CRITICAL. It was that a retained counter-example
named `test_ordinary_english_cannot_smuggle_a_false_claim_past_the_guard` **passed for the wrong
reason**: it looped over the seven words the fix had *deleted* from the exemption list, never one
that remained. A probe that iterates what a fix removed passes for free, forever, and reports the
hole as closed. Every probe added in response derives its inputs from the live data structure.

All 91 are dispositioned in `docs/process/OPEN_GATES.md` (`OG-94`…`OG-136`) and fixed in
`CHANGELOG.md` `[0.4.2]`. One — `OG-116` — was **built, measured, and rejected**: the fix produced
12 findings, 11 of them on correct prose, and is recorded with that measurement rather than shipped.

| id | sev | finding | measured | verifier |

---

## Pass 6 — Phase 3 deep verification, 2026-08-22, commit `c956619`

**11 agents, 0 errors, ~2.85M subagent tokens, 784 tool calls, 66 minutes.** **80 findings: 4 CRITICAL, 27 HIGH, 37 MEDIUM, 12 LOW.**

Verdicts by agent: PARTLY_REFUTED ×8, CONFIRMED ×2, REFUTED ×1.


This is the pass `HANDOFF.md` and `docs/plan/NEXT_SESSION.md` said had not run. It ran, against the
pushed commit, with every agent handed a **claim to attack** and never the diff, the live tree
read-only, and falsification confined to disposable `git archive` labs.

**It broke the claims.** One agent confirmed its claim outright, one refuted its claim outright, and
nine partly refuted. The single most valuable result: **`stamps` was reporting a false green on the
live tree** — found independently by three agents — on the very document a crashed session is told
to read first. The narrowing that closed the "blanket amnesty" hole in the same session had *moved*
the hole rather than closed it, which is `docs/process/RULES.md` Rule 1's sibling clause exactly.

Findings below are **verbatim from the agents, before any curation**, worst-first. Disposition lives
in `docs/process/OPEN_GATES.md`.

---

## Pass 5 — fresh-agent whole-system gate, weighted at the renderer, 2026-08-21, commit `494746c`

> **Numbering note added 2026-08-22.** The `OG-48`…`OG-58` keys opened by this pass collided with an earlier run that had already reached `OG-62`, so each named two findings (`OG-74`). The open ones were renumbered to `OG-63`…`OG-73`; **this log keeps its original numbers on purpose** — it is the verbatim record of what the pass wrote, and editing it to match a later renumbering would make it something else. The map is at the top of `docs/process/OPEN_GATES.md`.

**Shape.** Eight agents, zero memory of any prior fix, dispatched against a frozen commit. Seven
verified areas; the eighth judged release readiness. Each was handed a CLAIM to attack, never a
diff. Deliberately weighted at the **document renderer**, which four previous passes had never
exercised — every earlier pass verified the governance layer and the moat, and read the renderer's
source without ever rendering a document and looking at the result.

**Returned: 109 findings — 9 CRITICAL, 29 HIGH, 41 MEDIUM, 30 LOW.**

### Why the renderer was the richest seam

The engine's headline job is Markdown → self-contained HTML, and the suites could not see whether
it did that job. Nothing rendered a document and inspected the output, so *"30/30 tests pass"*
carried no information about output correctness. The flagship deliverable
`examples/capability-gallery.md` shipped with **118 literal backticks and 4 literal `**`** in its
visible body text, and **29 rendered paragraphs for 8 authored ones**, through four green passes.

This is `LESSONS.md` L-15 in its purest form — *a gate that certifies an artifact it never looks
at* — and it is the reason this pass was pointed where the green was thickest rather than where the
findings had previously been.

### Disposition of all 109

| outcome | count | notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Fixed this session, falsified red first** | 20 | 7 of 9 CRITICAL; 14 of 29 HIGH. See `CHANGELOG.md` `[0.3.5]`. |
| **Duplicates across agents** | ~24 | The renderer defects were found independently by three agents; the ECharts theme by two; the figure-id deletion by three. Independent rediscovery is signal, not noise — it is the closest thing to a second opinion this process has. |
| **Recorded with a disposition** | rest | `OG-48`…`OG-53` opened; the remainder fold into the existing `OG-47` doc-claim row, which is scheduled to Phase 3 by name. |
| **Still `BLOCKS_PHASE_3`** | **0** | Re-derived from `OPEN_GATES.md`, not asserted. |

### The two CRITICALs that were NOT renderer defects

- **The launch command in `START_NEXT_SESSION.md` was silently truncated.** It wrapped the prompt
  in double quotes while containing an inner `"import yaml"`, so the shell closed the string early:
  `claude` received a **365-character** prompt and a stray positional argument, discarding ~3,300
  characters. Four passes read that file; none ran the string through a shell parser. The
  replacement is generated and `shlex`-verified — one argument, full length — and re-verified by
  round-tripping it back out of the written document.
- **`install.sh --chain` silently skipped a non-executable predecessor** and reported a clean
  chain, switching off a foreign project's existing hook as a side effect of adopting ours.

### Disposition of every finding — the phase-gate accounting

Written after the second working session on this pass, so the counts below are the *final* state,
not the state at first triage. **Every one of the 109 is in exactly one row.**

| disposition | count | where it is recorded |
|---|---|---|
| **Fixed, each falsified red before green** | **41** | `CHANGELOG.md` `[0.3.5]`, by area |
| **Duplicate — same defect found by 2–3 agents independently** | **24** | folded into the fixed row above; rediscovery is signal, not noise |
| **NOT_A_DEFECT, with reasoning a verifier can attack** | **9** | `OG-51`, `OG-53`, and the four falsified below |
| **SCHEDULED to a phase whose `BUILD_PLAN.md` row names the work** | **7** | `OG-48` (Phase 4), `OG-49`, `OG-50` (Phase 3), plus the four pre-existing |
| **Folded into `OG-47`** — stale counts, dead citations, present-tense plans | **28** | `OG-47`, scheduled to Phase 3 by name; these are exactly what `counts`/`crossrefs`/`built-claims`/`present-tense` catch mechanically |
| **`BLOCKS_PHASE_3`** | **0** | — |

### Four findings that did NOT survive being checked

A verification pass is not a list of things to obey. These were reported against `494746c` and are
recorded as **not reproducible** with the evidence that killed them:

- **"`tools.health` exits 0 printing nothing if any check raises."** Falsified by injecting a
  raising check into the registry and running the real `main()`: it prints `[FAIL] … check raised
  RuntimeError`, `UNHEALTHY`, and **exits 1**. `run_all()` wraps every check individually and the
  wrapper recovers the documented check id from the function's source.
- **"A pie whose largest slice is 100% renders empty"** — true of docgen's **native** renderer
  (fixed: it emitted an arc whose start and end points coincide, which SVG omits entirely), **false
  of the ECharts engine**, which emits the standard two-arc full-circle idiom
  (`M400 96 A… 382.7 96.9 A… 400 96 Z`) and renders correctly.
- **"`--full` converting `STRUCT-06`'s deliberate SKIP into a failure defeats the reason the SKIP
  exists."** Not a defect: `--full` asserts *everything ran and passed*, and a check that cannot be
  answered in this environment is **unknown**. The engine's own output says it — *"a skip is
  unknown, not green"* — and treating unknown as green under the flag that means "run everything"
  is the failure, not the guard.
- **The `data-engine` CSS selector this session added to stop the blueprint grid showing through
  charts** matched nothing when first written, because no such attribute existed. Caught by
  asserting the selector's match count instead of assuming it; the attribute is now stamped by the
  renderer and 5 chart SVGs carry it in the flagship. Recorded because it is the *same* mistake the
  pass exists to catch, committed while fixing it.

### What this pass says about the method

Four passes at the governance layer converged toward zero findings and read as evidence the system
was sound. The first pass pointed somewhere new returned 109. **Convergence measured the pass, not
the system** — the classes were fixed where they were looked for. The durable lesson is in
`RULES.md` Rule 1's second clause: a counter-example must cover the **class**, not the instance.
The narrower form of the same mistake happened inside this session's own fixes: a duplicate-label
guard, correct for categorical charts, was applied to every family and broke the scatter golden on
the first run — caught only because the engine's own regression suite happened to hold a scatter.

---

## Pass 4 — BLIND adversarial claim-attack, 2026-08-20/21, commit `c9f61be`

**The method changed, and it is why this pass found what three others could not.** Eight independent
agents were each handed a **CLAIM about the repository and told to break it**. None was told that
anything had recently been fixed, none was shown a diff, and each was explicitly forbidden from
looking for one — because an agent told what was fixed verifies *the fix*, while an agent handed a
guarantee attacks *the guarantee*. Every earlier pass had been given the work to review.

**All 8 claims BROKEN. 67 findings — 12 CRITICAL, 17 HIGH, 27 MEDIUM, 11 LOW.**

The twelve CRITICALs de-duplicated to **four root causes**, not twelve bugs: the gate scanned a
filesystem *rendering* of the objects rather than the objects; the moat trusted untracked local
state; the integrity checks were structurally too weak; and the range computation was wrong. Fixing
the first class properly — read blob objects with `git cat-file` — closed six at once
(`LESSONS.md` **L-21**).

Three of the twelve were reachable **only by deliberately editing the hook**, which no client-side
hook can survive. Those were not patched; the *claim* was scoped instead, in
`docs/process/THREAT_MODEL.md` (`LESSONS.md` **L-22**).

*(The ninth agent, a completeness critic over the other eight, died on an account weekly limit
before returning. It is not represented here. Re-dispatching it is recorded as `OG-48`.)*

---

## Pass 3 — whole-system, 2026-08-20, commit `d12a13e`

**Scope:** the entire repository, not one phase's diff. **12 independent verifier agents**, none
of which saw the implementer's reasoning, dispatched against a frozen commit. Dimensions:
fresh-clone reality, gates-and-hooks, baseline-and-suites, claims-vs-reality, open-gates audit,
cross-document truth, data-file contracts / fail-closed mutation testing, gatekeeper topology,
modularity and extensibility, hygiene and repo state, next-phase readiness, and a completeness
critic over the other eleven.

**136 raw findings** — 8 CRITICAL, 29 HIGH,
58 MEDIUM, 41 LOW. Every one of the twelve returned `DEFECTS_FOUND`.

The CRITICALs de-duplicate to five distinct defects, **all five in the git moat that Phase 2 built**
— which had been reported complete, green, and falsified four separate ways one commit earlier.
That is the argument for this ritual, restated with evidence for the second phase running.

### CRITICAL (8)

| # | verifier | paths | finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| C01 | completeness-critic | `tools/hooks/install.sh`, `tools/hooks/pre-push`, `CHANGELOG.md` | The gate scans the ENDPOINT DIFF of the range (`git diff --name-only --diff-filter=d -z "$remote_sha" "$local_sha"`, line 94) and then archives the TIP commit (line 99). It never looks at the intermediate commits. Any secret introduced and then deleted or redacted by a later commit in the same push is published to the remote while the hook prints an AFFIRMATIVE clean line and exits 0. This is the most common way a secret reaches a remote: commit a key, notice, fix it, push. RESOLVES A DIRECT CONTRADICTION between two verifiers: fresh-clone-bootstrap tested only the secret-at-tip case and concl |
| C02 | completeness-critic | `tools/hooks/pre-push` | The exact defect the phase claims to have fixed is still live in the FALLBACK path. When the range cannot be resolved (remote sha absent from this clone — routine after a force-push race, in a shallow clone, or against a rewritten remote), line 119 runs `git archive HEAD` — the branch you are STANDING ON — instead of `$local_sha`, the ref actually being pushed. It then prints a positive 'secret scan clean (N pushed file(s))' over the wrong tree and exits 0. Same wrong-artifact class as LESSONS L-15, one code path over. |
| C03 | completeness-critic | `tools/hooks/pre-commit` | False for Gate 2. Only Gate 1 reads the index. A secret staged with `git add` and then overwritten in the working tree is committed while the hook prints an affirmative clean line. Composed with the endpoint-diff CRITICAL above, this puts a live credential on a remote with EVERY GATE GREEN AND NO BYPASS FLAG — which I proved end to end. |
| C04 | completeness-critic | `tools/health/checks.py`, `tools/hooks/install.sh`, `tools/hooks/pre-push` | 'which catches a stub' is false as measured — a stub is precisely what it does NOT catch. `_hook_check` (checks.py:169) is `marker not in text`, a substring search over the whole hook file, so a COMMENT satisfies it. | C06 | gatekeeper-topology | `HANDOFF.md` | `git commit --no-verify` and `git push --no-verify` bypass the entire moat silently, with exit 0 and no output, and the flag is documented NOWHERE in the tracked tree. | C08 | gates-and-hooks | `tools/hooks/pre-push` | The exact defect the last pass claimed to fix is still live in the FALLBACK path. A user who follows it literally after a fresh clone bootstraps and health-checks the wrong tree (or gets 'no such file or directory' if this machine's copy is absent), and would conclude the clone is fine without ever having run anything in it. |

Harmless to correctness, but --cached is a permanent no-op for the engine's own bundled documents. |

Fail-closed behaviour is unaffected (123 ≠ 0 → push refused). |

## Pass 2 — Phase 2 closeout, 2026-08-20, commit `0e5d1f5`

11 agents, 72 raw findings, **2 CRITICAL**: the pre-push secret gate read the working tree while
certifying pushed commits, and a cache hit bypassed a blocking gate. Both fixed in `d12a13e`; the
generalisations are `LESSONS.md` **L-15** and **L-16**. Pass 3 above then found that the first fix
was **narrower than believed** — it closed the secret-at-tip case only.

## Pass 1 — Phase 1 closeout, 2026-08-20, commit `cbd6807`

10 agents. Found a fabricated citation in the rulebook and a `CONS-03` lock-bypass, both fixed
across `ab8fad2`…`cbd6807`. **It did not end at zero findings** — it ended with 24 rows carried in
`OPEN_GATES.md`, which is what the register is for. No pass has ever been re-run against its own
fixed tree; Pass 3 was the first.
